#2902. Moritz Seider Sounds Like a Captain With Powerful Message After Red Wings Loss

The Detroit Red Wings were less than two minutes away from earning at least one point against the Vegas Golden Knights on Thursday night. Instead, another late-game breakdown left them empty-handed, and Moritz Seider wasn't interested in making excuses.

Following Detroit's heartbreaking 2-1 loss at Little Caesars Arena, the 25-year-old defenseman delivered a blunt message about what needs to change.

And considering the growing conversation surrounding Detroit's vacant captaincy, his words carried additional weight.

Nov 3, 2026; Tampa, Florida, USA; Detroit Red Wings defenseman Moritz Seider (53) plays the puck during the third period against Tampa Bay Lightning at Benchmark International Arena. Mandatory Credit: Pablo Robles-Imagn Images Credit: IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect (Reuters)

The Red Wings battled one of the NHL's strongest teams for nearly 60 minutes, but Trevor Connelly's goal with just 1:42 remaining in regulation handed Vegas the victory.

When asked what Detroit needs to improve in late-game situations, Seider delivered the following message in his postgame comments:

"We just got to be better. I think that's pretty simple. There are many aspects of the game that we need to focus on, but I think we just got to be real present in the moment and just have got to want it more than the other team."

Seider wasn't pointing fingers at a particular teammate or suggesting Detroit had played poorly all night. Instead, he was emphasizing the urgency and focus required to finish close games against quality opponents.

It's a message the Red Wings desperately need to hear.

Detroit had plenty of reasons to believe Thursday would end differently.

Daniil Tarasov delivered an outstanding performance, stopping 32 shots, including 14 in the opening period. Meanwhile, Anton Johansson gave the Red Wings a 1-0 lead early in the third period with his second goal of the season.

Unfortunately, that advantage disappeared just over two minutes later when Braeden Bowman tied the game for Vegas.

The score remained deadlocked until Connelly beat Tarasov in the closing minutes.

According to the NHL's official game recap, the winning goal developed when Connelly received a pass from Jack Eichel, used a toe-drag to create shooting space, and beat Tarasov with a wrist shot.

Head coach Todd McLellan acknowledged that Detroit had competed well enough to give itself an opportunity. But one late mistake ultimately decided a game that could have gone either way.

And that's precisely what makes Seider's comments important.

Detroit doesn't necessarily need to dominate every opponent for 60 minutes. It needs to execute when the game is on the line.

The timing of Seider's comments is particularly interesting because Red Wings fans have spent the past several days discussing whether he should become Detroit's next captain.

On Thursday morning, an unusual practice video sparked speculation that Seider could be receiving the captaincy.

Players reportedly waited several minutes for Seider before beginning drills, leading some fans to wonder whether something significant had happened inside the locker room.

There has been no official announcement, but Seider continues to demonstrate the qualities associated with a team captain.

He's willing to speak candidly after difficult losses, take responsibility for the team's shortcomings, and challenge his teammates to improve.

As we previously examined when discussing Detroit's captaincy decision, Seider has already established himself as one of the organization's most natural leadership candidates.

Thursday's comments only strengthen that argument.

Detroit now sits at 7-7-3 with 17 points, having lost two consecutive games and three of its past four.

The Red Wings will begin a four-game road trip Saturday against the Colorado Avalanche, giving them an immediate opportunity to respond to Thursday's disappointment.

But the bigger issue goes beyond one loss.

Detroit has repeatedly shown it can compete with quality opponents. The challenge is turning those competitive performances into victories.

My take: Seider's message is exactly what this team needs right now.

He's not asking teammates to perform miracles or pretending that Detroit can eliminate every mistake. He's demanding better execution, greater focus, and a stronger commitment when the outcome hangs in the balance.

Those are reasonable expectations for a team trying to establish itself as a legitimate playoff contender.

And whether or not Seider eventually receives the "C," he's already speaking like someone who understands the responsibility that comes with wearing it.

Now the Red Wings need to show they're listening.

#2894. Jaguars' point differential of +108 this season is 50 points better than any other team

No NFL team is dominating opponents like the Jacksonville Jaguars.

After blowing out the Ravens 33-10 on Thursday night, the Jaguars have now outscored their opponents by a cumulative score of 229-121 this season. That's a point differential of +108, by far the best in the NFL.

The team with the second-best point differential in the NFL is the 49ers, at +58. The Jaguars' point differential is 50 points better than the next-best team.

At 6-2, the Jaguars do not have the best record in the league, as the Bills, 49ers and Vikings are all 6-1. The 49ers are favored to beat the Raiders and improve to 7-1, and the Bills and Vikings play each other on Monday night, so the winner will be 7-1.

But at the midway point of the season, point differential is a better indication of a team's quality, and a better predictor of a team's future success, than win-loss record. That's because with as small a sample size as half a season, a few plays in a few close games can skew the won-loss record, but a few plays don't skew point differential. Historically, the teams that prove to be the best over the course of a season are the teams that win some blowouts, even if they also lose some close games.

Winning blowouts and losing close games is exactly what the Jaguars do. Both of the Jaguars' losses were one-possession games that were tied in the fourth quarter, while the Jaguars' wins have included some massive blowouts: The Jaguars' 35-point win over the Colts was the biggest blowout of this NFL season, their 29-point win over the Patriots was the fourth-biggest blowout in the NFL this season, their 24-point win over the Browns was the seventh-biggest blowout in the NFL this season, and Thursday night's 23-point win over the Ravens was the 11th-biggest blowout in the NFL this season.

The Jaguars have won four games by more than 21 points this season; no other team has more than one win by more than 21 points.

Jacksonville has a 3.5-game lead in the AFC South, and six of the Jaguars' nine remaining games are against opponents with a losing record. They're poised to run away with their division, perhaps earn the No. 1 seed in the AFC, and maybe blow some teams out in the playoffs just like they have in the regular season.

#2888. Joe Milton on his first start: The NFL is about being ready when your number is called

Joe Milton has never started an NFL game. The closest he came was in Week 18 of the 2024 season when Patriots starter Drake Maye played only three snaps before giving way to Milton, who played the final 61 snaps.

Milton looked like a starting NFL quarterback in that game, completing 22 of 29 passes for 241 yards and a touchdown.

Less than two years later, Milton is on his third team and has played only 51 snaps in four appearances with Dallas since.

He will make his first career start on Sunday when the Eagles play the Giants.

“I was just pretty much going through the plays, going through the week like I was a starter, or like I was playing, and just being ready,” Milton said Thursday, via Olivia Reiner of the Philadelphia Inquirer. “And that’s what the NFL is about: Just being ready when your name is called, and just preparing for those moments.”

The Eagles signed Milton off the Cowboys’ practice squad on Oct. 16 after they placed Jalen Hurts on injured reserve due to blood clots. Tanner McKee started the past three games, but went 0-3 as the Eagles ran their losing streak to six games.

Milton has had a crash course in learning the playbook and the terminology, which he said reminds him of the West Coast scheme he had in New England his rookie season.

#2885. What we learned as Steph Curry catches fire in second half as Warriors burn Heat

What we learned as Steph Curry catches fire in second half as Warriors burn Heat originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

SAN FRANCISCO – The team that had two days of rest looked much more like a team that played into overtime the night before in the early portions of the Warriors' game against the Miami Heat on Thursday night at Chase Center.

Slowly but surely, the Warriors found a second wind that wasn't available to the Heat, beating them 120-100. Their first lead of the game came from a Steph Curry layup at the 1:38 mark of the second quarter.

They outscored the Heat by 15 points in the third quarter and maintained the lead in the fourth quarter for a sixth straight win.

Curry continued his reign, scoring a game-high 29 points, with 22 coming after halftime. His running mate, Draymond Green, did a fantastic job defensively and engineering the Warriors' offense.

Former Warriors Klay Thompson (11 points) and Andrew Wiggins (two) combined for 13 points.

Here are three takeaways from the Warriors' win to keep rolling.

They shot on opposite sides of the court at the same time during their pregame routines and caught up for a moment together at half-court. The moment was just the start to what would be a long night for former longtime teammates Curry and Thompson. As the Heat won the opening tip, Curry went right to Thompson for his defensive matchup.

That was the area Thompson made an immediate impact, too. He played so well on Brandin Podziemski that Steve Kerr had to make a change faster than usual. Thompson even swatted Curry at the rim in the first quarter after getting beat by him on a back cut.

Neither got it going offensively in the first half. Curry scored just seven points on a lowly 2-of-10 shooting, and a three late in the second quarter were Thompson's only points. Curry splashed a three within the first minute of the third quarter, but also fouled Thompson on a three the next time down.

But the third quarter has been all Curry's this season, and he continued his tear against his former Splash Brother. Curry scored 14 points and made three 3-pointers in the quarter to help the Warriors secure a 15-point lead going into the fourth.

Curry now is shooting 61.9 percent from three in the third quarter (13 of 21). He added another eight points in the fourth quarter to fall one point shy of his fourth straight 30-point game.

When Green came apart last game and was ejected against the Suns, the Warriors came together to complete a comeback win. There were many more storylines outside of Green going into the game, but he was sure to get up for a game matching up against Giannis Antetokounmpo and Co.

Green dared Antetokounmpo to shoot from deep and was active around the rim, whether it was with the former Milwaukee Bucks superstar, his former Warriors teammate Andrew Wiggins or anybody else. His level of focus was night-and-day different from Monday night.

This was a game where you had to watch closely to understand Green's impact. Look beyond the box score with him. But some numbers certainly stick out, too.

Antetokounmpo scored 20 points but was a minus-22. Green's plus-13 was second among Warriors starters, behind just Curry, and his 11 assists were five more than his next closest teammate. The Warriors had a 96.2 defensive rating, and Green is the main reason why.

Sure, Bam Adebayo put him on roller skates in the second quarter, but Al Horford got him back in the third quarter and ultimately had the final laugh in another Warriors win.

And, that wasn't even Horford's best highlight from the game. Or the quarter, for that matter.

Horford, 40, flew in for a two-handed reverse tip-in layup that made him look closer to his jersey number (20) than his age.

The 20-year vet has scored more points this season than he did Thursday night. He's grabbed more rebounds and made more threes. But in his strategic minutes, he hasn't meant as much to the Warriors as he did against the Heat, finishing as a game-high plus-21 in a game when he was a perfect 3 of 3 from the field and 2 of 2 from deep.

#2882. Drake Maye’s early-season turnaround earns him October honors

New England Patriots quarterback Drake Maye was named AFC Offensive Player of the Month for October.

The award comes after Maye led the Patriots to a 3-1 record, completing 67.2% of his passes for 1,199 yards, 12 touchdowns and just two interceptions. He also added 129 yards and two touchdowns on the ground.

Maye became the first Patriot to earn the honor since Tom Brady during the 2017 season.

"That's something to be proud of for Drake," Patriots offensive coordinator Josh McDaniels said. "I always look at those things as team achievements. Certainly nobody's going to win an individual award in football without all the guys that do their jobs around him. I think you celebrate that as a group.

"He's certainly deserving of it, and a lot of credit to him for just kind of improving as September finished and got into the next month of the season. A lot of little things he did better, and hopefully we can continue to do that."

It marked a significant turnaround for Maye after the 24-year-old struggled to open the season. The quarterback was perhaps the biggest reason the Patriots found themselves with a 1-2 record through the first three weeks, throwing just one touchdown against six interceptions.

Improved ball security has been one of the biggest factors in Maye's resurgence. He has thrown just one interception over the last four weeks, while his 1.2% turnover-worthy play rate in October ranked third among qualified quarterbacks.

Additionally, Maye's trademark deep ball has returned despite the Patriots losing several of their top pass catchers to injuries, while has has operated as one of the league's better quarterbacks when dealing with pressure.

"He has worked really diligently for two years now on becoming a better passer from the pocket," McDaniels said. "Everybody talks about how his athleticism is, and it's great, and he made another great play outside the pocket last week. But I'd say he'd probably be more excited about the one that he slid to the right and climbed up into the pocket, and makes the throw down the field to Kyle [Williams]. So I think he's worked really hard at it.

"I think it's repetition and I think it's confidence in the protections. I think that's where it stems from."

Maye's November started strong as well as he scored three total touchdowns to lead a come from behind win in Miami. The quarterback will look to keep things going against the Green Bay Packers this Sunday.

#2881. Game Preview: The spiraling Suns need a win tonight in the worst way

Who: Phoenix Suns (1-5) vs. Orlando Magic (2-5)

Where: Mortgage Matchup Center — Phoenix, Arizona

Watch: Arizona's Family 3TV, Arizona's Family Sports

The Suns are back at it tonight after a loss to the Trail Blazers last night. The homestand continues.

Four straight losses. 1-5. And the Suns just watched Portland drop 43 on them in a single quarter after winning the first one 34-29.

I'll say it out loud: game 7 of an 82-game season is a must-win. That sounds insane from the outside. It isn't. Houston and San Antonio come to town right after this, and if Phoenix can't beat a 2-5 Orlando team missing Paolo Banchero, I have no idea where the next win comes from.

Through six games, nothing on film says this team is better than its record. The ball movement in the first quarter in the last two games showed what the ceiling looks like. NBA games are 48 minutes long; it needs to be there for more than 12 or 15 minutes. If Jordan Ott's group can't defend a bottom-10 offense at home, the conversation shifts from "early-season growing pains" to "roster construction problem," and the lineup experimentation has to start now.

Mark Williams — OUT (Left Shoulder Surgery)

Jordan Goodwin — OUT (Right Calf Soreness)

Rasheer Fleming — QUESTIONABLE (Left Ankle Sprain)

The Suns need to play big, for once. Orlando has size.

Phoenix got out-rebounded by 8 against Portland and let the Blazers shoot 41 free throws. Donovan Clingan put up 24 and 11 inside. Portland was the more physical team, and the Suns settled instead of attacking. That's a size problem and a mentality problem at the same time.

Orlando brings real size back. Franz Wagner, Desmond Bane and Wendell Carter Jr. have a 105.9 defensive rating together over 155 minutes, which is elite. But the Magic are running a switch-heavy scheme with a small rookie guard in the starting five, and that's the mismatch Phoenix should live on.

Bench depth matters more than usual. It's the second night of a home-home back-to-back. Orlando is fresh from rest and starting a four-game road trip

Phoenix's defense has to show up. They are ranked 30th in the NBA in defensive rating at 124.5. The Magic have been held to under 100 points four times this season already. Orlando is 30th in PPG and 23rd in Offensive Rating.

Correcting avoidable and "controllable" mistakes will be key in this one, and every game moving forward for that matter.

The Suns' defense will be instrumental in turning this entire thing around, but especially this game. A struggling Magic squad could be just what the doctor ordered. But Orlando is thinking the same thing about the Suns' defense being what they need to get their slumping offense back on track.

Set the tone defensively. Rebound the basketball. Play connected. And most importantly…

Play confident! These stretches can make you second-guess or lose confidence in yourself individually, or as a team. Phoenix needs to do all it can to block out all the noise and get back to focusing on the basics and taking it one game at a time.

Protect your home floor. Get win number two. Build some momentum heading into a tough 2-game stretch against the Rockets and Spurs after this one. The alternative… is not fun to think about.

I've predicted against them in just about every game thus far, so it's time to flip the "hope" switch on and speak their second win into existence.

#2880. Derrick Henry moves to seventh on the NFL's all-time rushing list

The Ravens haven’t had much to celebrate Thursday night, but Derrick Henry gave the home fans something to cheer.

Henry had 51 first-half rushing yards, which was enough to move him into seventh on the all-time rushing list. He passed Jerome Bettis (13,662) and LaDainian Tomlinson (13,684) in moving from ninth to seventh.

Curtis Martin is next on the list, with his 14,101 career rushing yards ranking sixth all time.

Henry entered the night with 632 yards for the season and 13,650 in his career.

Over the past four seasons, Henry has averaged 1,578 yards. He has averaged 85 yards per game in his career.

Henry will need to play another three to four years to break Emmitt Smith’s record of 18,355 yards.

Smith was 35 years old in his final season of 2004. Henry turns 33 in January.

Smith said earlier this year that he believes Henry has a chance to break his record.

Across a combined 25 regular-season games the past two seasons with the then-South Bay Lakers — who have since relocated and rebranded — Bronny James posted 18.4 points, 4.4 assists, 4 rebounds and 1.5 steals per game while shooting 49.6% from the field, including 41.3% from beyond the arc.

James, now 22, was the No. 55 pick when he was drafted out of USC more than two years ago. He appeared in 27 regular-season games with the Lakers as a rookie, making father-son history but struggling mightily at times, particularly early on.

He made noticeable progress in Year 2, taking the court for 42 regular-season games and raising his field-goal percentage from 31.3% (2024-25) to 40.9% (2025-26) and his 3-point percentage from 28.1% (2024-25) to 38.6% (2025-26).

Head coach JJ Redick saw enough from James to include him in his playoff rotation last season, albeit a shorthanded one.

That paved the way for additional excitement and milestones.

Coming into Year 3, the question was: How would James fare without his superstar father on the Lakers (including how would he be treated by staff and teammates in the absence of the future Hall of Famer)?

But James hasn't been given much of an opportunity in the regular season to make any kind of jump. In his sparing minutes, he scored 0.8 points per game. He attempted only three field goals and made two of them.

James could wind up back with the Lakers soon, but his lack of on-court involvement so far isn't promising, in terms of his chances of carving out the long-term 3-and-D role he teased on occasion last season.

As for Knecht, he ended up signing a two-way deal with the New York Knicks, for whom he logged 10 points on 4-of-6 shooting, plus seven rebounds, on Wednesday in a win over the Chicago Bulls.

#2874. George Kittle is skeptical that Brandon Aiyuk will "get in" the team facility

As 49ers receiver Brandon Aiyuk attempts to lay the foundation for a return to the NFL, at least one other 49ers player has expressed skepticism about Aiyuk’s plan.

His new agent, Drew Rosenhaus, has said that Aiyuk will report to the 49ers’ facility on Friday. Tight end George Kittle shared his views regarding that strategy on Thursday when asked whether he’s looking forward to seeing Aiyuk.

“Do you think he gets in the facility?” Kittle said, via Grant Cohn of SI.com.

Kittle then was asked if he thinks Aiyuk gets into the facility.

“Plead the fifth,” Kittle said. “Yeah. You can ask [G.M.] John [Lynch]. Or [coach] Kyle [Shanahan]. Don’t ask me. I’m a conspiracy guy.”

“Is there a conspiracy?” Kittle was asked.

Kittle’s skepticism is warranted. The entire situation is strange, and awkward. Aiyuk clearly hopes to trigger his outright release by reporting for work, after months of making it clear he had no intention to do so.

The 49ers are tiptoeing around the situation, for now. Before the team has to do anything, Aiyuk needs to check the various boxes that put the 49ers in a posture that requires a decision.

Of all potential outcomes, the least likely involves Aiyuk being welcomed back onto the roster. Especially since Aiyuk likely has no desire to be on the roster.

He wants his freedom, and he finally — after months of stubbornness — has initiated the process aimed at making that happen. While it’s possible that the 49ers will search for a hardball outcome that involves keeping him from becoming a free agent while avoiding putting him back on the team, the chances of Aiyuk wandering around the locker room and eventually wearing a 49ers’ helmet again seem to be incredibly slim.

#2873. Justin Jefferson says his frustration came from "wanting to win"

Vikings receiver Justin Jefferson engaged in a rare display of frustration late in Sunday’s 19-15 loss to the Lions. He also didn’t talk to reporters after the game, possibly to avoid saying things that would underscore what he was feeling in the moment.

Four days later, Jefferson spoke to the media — and the No. 1 topic was his apparent mood during Minnesota’s first loss of the season.

“It was wanting to win,” Jefferson said, via Kevin Seifert of ESPN, “and I felt like we had plenty of opportunities to win that game and we only hurt ourselves in multiple ways. It’s just wanting better from the whole entire offense especially. And I feel like the potential and the guys that we have in that room is something very, very special. I feel like we’re wasting that if not going out there executing the plays that we have called and just not living to that standard that we feel like we live up to every single day.”

Jefferson, who always says all the right things about his quarterbacks, said all the right things about Kyler Murray, whose performance against Detroit largely contributed to the failure to score more than 15 points.

“I feel like Kyler has continued to grow,” Jefferson said. “I feel like Kyler is continuing to get comfortable with this offense. And I feel like the plays that are there are plays that he’s well capable of making. And those are plays that we’ve seen him make multiple times throughout the season, throughout practice and in training camp as well. So we definitely have faith in him. We have confidence in him to go out there and be that guy that we need him and want him to be.

“But all in all, it’s just executing our plays and just coming ready to play. And I feel like once he’s back out there and he gets more comfortable with the system, more comfortable with just throwing to these different type of receivers, then I feel like he’s going to look a lot better in [reporters’] eyes. But we see that every single day. We see that type of Kyler that we need out there on Sundays every single day. So just bringing that on Sundays. We’re behind him. We’re confident in him, so we just going to keep riding with him.”

For now, the Vikings will ride with Carson Wentz, while Murray is out with a hamstring injury. And Wentz has proven that, of all the quarterbacks who have played for the Vikings since Sam Darnold left for Seattle after the 2024 season, Wentz knows how to get the ball to Jefferson.

Soft bench or not, Wentz has an opportunity to stay on the field if he makes the plays that Murray didn’t on Sunday. Plays that, by the time the game was on the line, coach Kevin O’Connell didn’t allow Murray to try to make.

The one moment that resonates from Sunday’s game wasn’t anything Murray did or failed to do. It came from O’Connell’s decision to punt from the Minnesota 33-yard line on fourth and five with 3:01 to play. Given the manner in which the Lions were running the ball, the likelihood of getting another opportunity to score a touchdown was slim.

Put simply, if O’Connell didn’t trust Murray to gain five yards with the game on the line, O’Connell shouldn’t entrust the starting job to Murray.

For now, Murray technically can’t play due to injury. It’s hard not to wonder whether, if Murray had made the plays needed to outscore the Lions on Sunday, he’d be playing on Monday night despite the injured hamstring.

Napoli vs Lazio | Where to Watch, Insights, Lineups & Prediction

#2871. How to watch Warriors vs. Heat

The Golden State Warriors have reached the halfway point of their six-game homestand as they prepare to host the Miami Heat on Thursday night. Tip-off is scheduled for 7:00 PM PT in San Francisco and can be watched on NBA TV and NBC Sports Bay Area.

Golden State extended its winning streak to five games on Monday night with a thrilling 116-113 victory over the Phoenix Suns. Stephen Curry and Draymond Green returned to the lineup after sitting out the first game of the back-to-back for rest, however, Green's night was cut short after he was ejected early in the third quarter following a scuffle with Suns players Khaman Maluach and Dillon Brooks.

With Green out, the Warriors once again leaned on Curry to carry the offensive load, and the superstar guard delivered. Curry finished with a team-high 34 points, including an explosive 18-point third quarter that helped Golden State secure its fifth consecutive victory. The Warriors also received some much-needed contributions from their supporting cast as Brandin Podziemski added 20 points, while Charles Bassey and rookie Yaxel Lendeborg provided a spark with 19 and 16 points, respectively.

Thursday night's matchup against Miami brings another familiar face back to Chase Center as Warriors legend Klay Thompson returns to face his former team. After reaching a buyout agreement with the Dallas Mavericks this offseason, Thompson signed a two-year deal with the Heat. The former Splash Brother has settled into his new role in Miami, averaging 14.1 points per game while shooting 42.5% from beyond the arc. He showed this as recently as Wednesday night, knocking down the clutch go-ahead three-pointer with just one second remaining to lift Miami past the Los Angeles Clippers in overtime.

There's always a little extra energy in the building whenever Thompson returns to the Bay Area, but with Golden State riding a five-game winning streak and Miami coming off a dramatic victory of their own, Thursday's matchup should have plenty of intensity as both teams look to keep their momentum going.

#2870. The July 2026 Red Sox were never out of the game… even when fans were

Before we begin looking ahead to 2027, OTM is looking back at 2026, highlighting some of the biggest, strangest, and most memorable moments of a memorable season. Today, we look at an unlikely comeback in the middle of the streak.

A few days ago, before the Ravens-Bills matchup, ESPN ran a parody ad on folks who left a 2025 game early, as Josh Allen and the Bills found themselves down two possessions in the fourth quarter. The Bills, to the ire of this Patriots fan, would win that primetime game last year, and Josh Allen, never one to mince words in his postgame interviews, said "Our team didn't quit. I mean, I think there's people that left the stadium. That's OK. We'll be fine. But have some faith next time." The camera then cut to some folks who were in the parking lot during Allen's comeback, and I found myself laughing at Buffalo fans, as is often easy to do amongst their deep playoff runs filled with hope, their chirps that at least Allen would win an MVP, and celebrations of October records. Of course they'd leave this game early, and how funny it was that they weren't able to see how brilliant their quarterback was in the clutch in person!

I then recoiled in horror as I realized that, just four months ago, on one fateful afternoon much further downstate in Flushing, I did the exact same thing to our beloved Red Sox.

In my first year of being a resident of the Capital Region, New York, it has been easier for me to hit the Big Apple than Boston. I circled the Mets-Red Sox series in my calendar for the middle of July, reluctantly, as the Red Sox did not start the season very well and the game would be directly before the All-Star game. By the day of the game, though, I was very bullish on this Red Sox team. The Red Sox were winners of eight consecutive games, and it didn't much matter if those wins came against the Angels and the White Sox, the latter of whom, at the time, weren't the formidable contender they ended up becoming later in the season, but were certainly no slouches. The team had awakened offensively against the Angels and was showing definite signs of life

This was not the case, though, in the early innings of this game against the Mets, after my trip down 87. The Red Sox had trouble getting anything going against a lanky lefty Zac Thornton. Thornton ended up being a bright spot in an otherwise hapless season for the Mets, but he was on the mound for the first time in what was already his third call-up of the season from Triple-A Syracuse. His first try resulted in the Nationals taking him for five runs in 4 1/3 innings. It was fine for the offensive to flounder while I was watching – I had seen it plenty of times this season. But did the bats have to flounder so harshly while the wonderful Obsession star Inde Navarette was in attendance? No, no, no, no, no, don't do that! I thought we were having a nice win streak!

If matters weren't bad enough, this was a scheduled short appearance from Payton Tolle, who looked serviceable for a bit longer, striking out Juan Soto with authority (you can watch that clip below) and Brayan Bello, the guy who departed the Major League roster about a month prior to fall in love with baseball again, was up in relief. Frankly, I was writing this game up as a loss mentally, as the Mets lineup did not look punchless so far and Tolle was lucky to have just Francisco Lindor's RBI double after 3 2/3 innings.

But Bello coasted for quite a while. Lindor would be pesky yet again with a home run to make the game 2-0, but that was one of two batters Bello did not retire, and the other one would be victim to a double play, anyway. Bello would pitch through the eighth. The problem, though, is that Thornton was dealing. Through seven, he had given up just two hits, was pitching a shutout, and had struck out five. But, good things can't last forever, and so interim manager Andy Green went to Luke Weaver to start the eighth. At that point, with how hollow the Red Sox's hitting attempts had become, the Red Sox's expected winning percentage was hovering around 7 percent. The Red Sox would go 1-2-3 in the top of the eighth. More of the same.

Enter Devin Williams, and enter my walk to the exit when, after a Ceddanne Rafaela single, he'd unceremoniously retire Abreu with a pop fly. The team's pop was completely nonexistent; why would I subject myself to sitting in more NYC traffic after a loss? It was a sure thing, right? No sooner did my feet hit the closing gate than I heard a roar from a rather divided crowd. Francisco Lindor, the guy who was singlehandedly responsible for the lead the Mets had built, had bobbled the most routine of grounders that would have ended the game. The Red Sox now had men on first and second, and I was left to turn the stream on in the car.

The rest is history as we know it: Devin Williams would walk in the Red Sox's first run of the game. Aroldis Chapman would start warming, Jarren Duran would have a clutch RBI knock, the game would go to the bottom of the 9th inning all tied up at 2, Chapman would be every bit of the historically dependable reliever we've come to know to send the game to extras, the top of the tenth would see the Red Sox playing small ball extremely efficiently, scoring the Manfred runner Masataka Yoshida on two consecutive plays. The small ball was a welcome addition to their game plan, and on that Sunday, it was due to their trust in the man who'd come in to close the game out, Garrett Whitlock, who slammed the door the same way I slammed my door when I arrived to it in the parking lot.

The Red Sox were winners of nine consecutive games going into the All-Star break, a streak that would last two more series. Even better, they were in that situation thanks to contributions — 13 outs in relief, to be exact — from a guy who was one of the reasons the season starting so dire in the first place. On July 12, Bello was electric, and so began a summer that made it exciting to root for Brayan Bello's growth and success again. They were finding ways to win. I guess, as Josh Allen put it so eloquently, when dealing with a team I should be confident in, I'll have more faith next time.

Have you ever left a game early and really wish you hadn't? Can anyone one-up me with a 2013 ALDS story? Sound off below!

#2869. Jaguars' point differential of +108 this season is 50 points better than any other team

No NFL team is dominating opponents like the Jacksonville Jaguars.

After blowing out the Ravens 33-10 on Thursday night, the Jaguars have now outscored their opponents by a cumulative score of 229-121 this season. That’s a point differential of +108, by far the best in the NFL.

The team with the second-best point differential in the NFL is the 49ers, at +58. The Jaguars’ point differential is 50 points better than the next-best team.

At 6-2, the Jaguars do not have the best record in the league, as the Bills, 49ers and Vikings are all 6-1. The 49ers are favored to beat the Raiders and improve to 7-1, and the Bills and Vikings play each other on Monday night, so the winner will be 7-1.

But at the midway point of the season, point differential is a better indication of a team’s quality, and a better predictor of a team’s future success, than win-loss record. That’s because with as small a sample size as half a season, a few plays in a few close games can skew the won-loss record, but a few plays don’t skew point differential. Historically, the teams that prove to be the best over the course of a season are the teams that win some blowouts, even if they also lose some close games.

Winning blowouts and losing close games is exactly what the Jaguars do. Both of the Jaguars’ losses were one-possession games that were tied in the fourth quarter, while the Jaguars’ wins have included some massive blowouts: The Jaguars’ 35-point win over the Colts was the biggest blowout of this NFL season, their 29-point win over the Patriots was the fourth-biggest blowout in the NFL this season, their 24-point win over the Browns was the seventh-biggest blowout in the NFL this season, and Thursday night’s 23-point win over the Ravens was the 11th-biggest blowout in the NFL this season.

The Jaguars have won four games by more than 21 points this season; no other team has more than one win by more than 21 points.

Jacksonville has a 3.5-game lead in the AFC South, and six of the Jaguars’ nine remaining games are against opponents with a losing record. They’re poised to run away with their division, perhaps earn the No. 1 seed in the AFC, and maybe blow some teams out in the playoffs just like they have in the regular season.

#2867. Derrick Henry's production has plummeted without Lamar Jackson this season

Without quarterback Lamar Jackson, the Ravens want to be able to rely on running back Derrick Henry. That plan isn’t working.

In Thursday night’s 33-10 loss to the Jaguars, Henry managed just 57 yards on 17 carries. That continues what has been happening all season, with Henry struggling to get anything going without Jackson on the field.

It was a bad omen in Week 4 against the Titans, when Jackson left at halftime with an ankle injury and Henry was ineffective in the second half. In the first half of that game, with Jackson on the field, Henry had seven carries for 38 yards, an average of 5.4 yards per carry, and a touchdown. In the second half with Tyler Huntley, Henry had 16 carries for 35 yards, an average of 2.2 yards per carry, and no touchdowns.

Jackson has played one snap since that Titans game, and the story has been the same, with Henry not able to do much of anything.

Overall this season, in three and a half games playing with Jackson, Henry has 73 carries for 339 yards, an average of 4.6 yards per carry and 97 yards per game, and seven touchdowns. In five and a half games without Jackson, Henry has 102 carries for 350 yards, an average of 3.4 yards per carry and 64 yards per game, and one touchdown.

At 4-5 and in last place in the AFC North, the Ravens, who entered the year with Super Bowl aspirations, are on the verge of their season collapsing. They need to turn things around, and they need Henry to turn things around.

#2866. 'I’m worried' - Jose Mourinho sounds alarm bells for Real Madrid ahead of grueling Valencia showdown

Jose Mourinho insists he is not worried by the upcoming international break despite Real Madrid prepare to face Valencia in Liga. The Portuguese manager is instead fully focused on the threat posed by a fresh and determined Valencia side at Mestalla.

Mourinho has dismissed concerns regarding the recent international window ahead of Madrid's return to Liga action. The Portuguese manager is preparing his squad for a demanding trip to face Valencia at Mestalla. Los Blancos will take to the pitch on Saturday evening for their matchday 12 fixture. Many of Mourinho's key players will spend the next two weeks traveling and playing for their respective countries.

However, the Madrid boss insists that those international commitments are not his primary focus right now. His full attention is firmly fixed on securing a positive result against a dangerous domestic opponent.

The overriding concern for Mourinho is the physical advantage Valencia might hold this weekend. He highlighted that their upcoming opponents have enjoyed an uninterrupted period to get ready for the high-stakes clash. Addressing the media at the Ciudad Real Madrid, Mourinho made his immediate priorities clear.

"I'm worried about tomorrow's match, worried in a manner of speaking," Mourinho explained on the club's official website. "The difficulty of tomorrow's match is my concern. The national team isn't a problem. That coaches respect the players, the minutes they give them, and the protection against possible injuries."

While club managers often lament the international break, Mourinho offered a surprisingly measured take on the situation. He stressed that representing a national side remains a proud achievement for his players, provided they are managed safely.

"It doesn't worry me at all that a player has the honor of playing for his country," he added. "For me, the problem is tomorrow's match against a fresh team that had the whole week to prepare, with a coach who isn't concerned about whether they'll play beautifully or not, but about winning the game at all costs. It's a very tough match, and that's what concerns me."

A win is a must for Madrid as they face Valencia this weekend. Mourinho's side currently sits third in the Liga standings with 27 points from 11 matches - three points behind league leaders Barcelona. Meanwhile, the Blaugrana are set to take on second-placed Atletico Madrid in another fixture, which could present an opportunity for Los Blancos to turn up the heat at the top of the table.

#2865. Kon Knueppel injury update: Hornets reveal forward has hamstring strain

The Charlotte Hornets seemingly got the exact news they did not want.

Not even six minutes into his regular season debut, Rookie of the Year runner up Kon Knueppel left a game Wednesday, Nov. 4 against the Brooklyn Nets, with what the team then called left leg soreness.

On the latest injury report that published Thursday, Nov. 5, however, the team confirmed that it was a left hamstring strain, which was the same injury that sidelined him for the season's first eight games. The Hornets also already declared Knueppel out for their game Friday, Nov. 6 against the Boston Celtics.

The injury took place midway through the first quarter of Charlotte's game against Brooklyn.

Although there didn't appear to be a specific play in which Knueppel got hurt, he exited shortly after he dribbled the ball off his foot. During the play, Knueppel tried to dribble the ball behind his back but it caromed off his right leg, forcing him to take a small step as he tried to recover it. Immediately after the play, Knueppel limped but tried to stay in the game before quickly heading to the bench. He eventually went into the locker room and did not return.

After the game, Hornets coach Charles Lee told the local broadcast that he did not have an update on Knueppel and said he would be evaluated once the team returned to Charlotte later Wednesday.

Knueppel scored 3 points, all from free throws, missed his only shot attempt – a 3-pointer – and committed 2 turnovers in 5:51 on the floor against the Nets. He did not record any other statistic.

"Hopefully I can contribute in a capacity similar to last year, and with all the offseason work that I put in, even more," Knueppel told the Hornets broadcast in a pregame interview. "It might take me a little bit to get to that full capability, but tonight I'll be ready to go and help us get a win."

Last season, his first in the NBA after the Hornets selected him with the No. 4 overall pick in the 2025 NBA Draft, Knueppel recorded 18.5 points, 5.3 rebounds and 3.4 assists per game. He converted 3-pointers at a 42.5% clip and lost out to his college teammate at Duke, Cooper Flagg, for the Rookie of the Year honor.

#2864. Washington Commanders vs LA Rams Week 9: Five Questions with Turf Show Times

It's week 9 of the NFL season and the 2-4-1 Washington Commanders will be facing a 4-4 Rams team at home at Northwest Stadium on Sunday at 1:00 p.m. ET. The Rams started the season hyped as the best team in the NFL and presented as likely Super Bowl winners, but have struggled with consistency since then. At the same time, the Rams are a talented and well-coached team, having blown out the Giants 28-6 in Week 2 and the Chargers 24-9 last week.

On offense, the Rams are coached by head coach Sean McVay, offensive coordinator Nate Scheelhaase, and assistant HC Kliff Kingsbury, with McVay calling the plays. Two of those three coaches should be familiar to Washington fans as they were former playcallers here. This offense is built around one of the NFL's best offensive lines, a gap-scheme run game that favors power, and the NFL's highest use of 3-TE sets. It's an offense that sets up to bludgeon opposing defenses in the run game, but also uses their heavy personnel tendency to force opposing defenses to go heavy and set up star WR Puka Nacua to feast on heavier, slower defensive matchups. This kind of offense funnels the ball to Nacua, who currently leads the NFL with a 37.9% target share. A ruptured hamstring by WR Davante Adams will only increase the likelihood of the Rams going heavy and throwing the ball to Nacua. Overall, the Rams have the 3rd-ranked passing offense and the 6th-ranked rushing offense in terms of yards per game.

On defense, the Rams have been led by DC Chris Shula, who has worked his way up from being the assistant linebackers coach of the Rams in 2017. Shula has typically employed a 3-4 front with heavy use of zone coverage on the backend, though he is also known for extensive use of disguise, both on his pressure packages (faking a blitz from one direction while actually bringing pressure from another), and in his coverage (rotating coverages post-snap to confuse QBs). This year, after the Rams traded a 1st-round pick for shutdown CB Trent McDuffie, they have occasionally been using him to shadow star WRs, which allowed him to erase Malik Nabers in their Week 2 matchup. However, McDuffie's status is uncertain this week after sustaining a back injury against the Chargers. The Rams also just lost star CB Jaylen Watson to a season-ending shoulder injury, which could leave the backend of the Rams defense weakened. At the same time, Los Angeles may be getting back star pass rusher Myles Garrett this week, which would be a tremendous boon for their defense. Overall, the Rams have run one of the NFL's best defenses this year by many metrics, though it has ranked better against the pass (7th) than run (15th).

I asked Kenneth Arthur of Turf Show Times five questions to better understand the state of the Rams and what to look for in this game.

1) The Rams entered the season widely considered an elite team and Super Bowl favorite, but sit at 4-4 and on the outside looking in to win their division. Why has their season started off much slower than expected?

I'd start with revisiting why the Rams were considered such heavy favorites. A perennial contender under Sean McVay, the reigning MVP, then they add Myles Garrett, Trent McDuffie, and Aaron Donald, right? Garrett hasn't played since Week 1. McDuffie has missed a couple of games. Donald isn't more than a rotational option at this point in his career.

But that doesn't explain everything, right? Well, the Rams are the perfect example of an "Achilles" or a "Goliath". They look dangerous but they always have the same weak points. I wasn't convinced the Rams were Super Bowl favorites going into the season because they didn't fix special teams, they didn't use the draft to make their team better now, and they have too many players who are on the precipice of another injury, such as Puka Nacua, Davante Adams, and Matthew Stafford. Nacua has been banged up, Adams is out 6-10 weeks, and Stafford is dealing with elbow soreness. Jaylen Watson, another big free agent addition, has also been banged up and aggravated a shoulder injury on Sunday.

Special teams is awful, the receiver corps is thin, and the defense has predictable holes at linebacker. The defensive line was further weakened with injuries to Kobie Turner and Poona Ford.

Overall, it's a banged up team with bad special teams and they were very close to wins over the Broncos and Bills anyway. I think the bigger concern is losing to the Cardinals. However, Garrett is returning to practice this week, so it's possible we see the closest thing to the version of the Rams people thought they'd be when those predictions were made.

2) The Rams have had a disastrous performance on special teams so far this year. What are some of the specific problems the Rams special teams have faced, how are they dealing with them, and what do you think they will be like going forward?

The Rams have had bad special teams almost since McVay took over. He fired popular coordinator John Fassell a few years into the job and it's been downhill since then. L.A. won't focus any effort on finding a reliable kicker. They had Matt Gay and then let him walk in free agency over money. Now they're on their third kicker–but last week they had TWO active kickers, so they're almost on their fourth. Not locking down a good kicker is a big reason that the magnifying glass is on L.A.'s special teams.

Kickoff coverage has always been weak and the Rams don't even really try to understand the new rules. They are 27th in kickoff yard average and 32nd in punts that land inside the 20. Punter Ethan Evans has a big leg but won't be known for technique or accuracy, nor does he have good coverage. Special teams coordinator Bubba Ventrone (fired by the Broncos last season) is just the latest in a long line of people to have that job. He probably won't last long either.

The Rams have signed a few special teamers during the year in an effort to improve, like Grant Stuard and Alex Bachman, but it's not much and not having an impact.

They will continue to be bad moving forward. That's what I predict.

3) The Rams have been really efficient in the draft, with late-round gems like Puka Nacua, but at the same time have seemed to devalue high draft picks and preferred to trade them away for known NFL quantities. What do you think of this approach of GM Les Snead in recent years with respect to draft picks and roster construction?

I would disagree that the Rams have been "efficient", or even successful, in the draft. I think Puka Nacua is doing all the heavy lifting in that argument, but I'll give two more: Kyren Williams in the 5th and Quentin Lake in the 6th in 2022.

L.A. drafted three starting quality players on day two in 2023 (Byron Young, Steve Avila, Turner), and then RT Warren McClendon and Nacua on day three, and a couple of role players. That's all fine.

The biggest issues start in 2024, which is why the Rams are so thin at certain positions now. First-round pick Jared Verse was very overrated and that's proving out in Cleveland. Second-round pick Braden Fiske (the Rams traded a future second-round pick to move up for Fiske, one of the biggest day two overpays in history) has regressed. Third-round picks Blake Corum and Kam Kinchens are "OK". Then the Rams made six day three picks and none of them are consequential players, if they're even still on the team.

The 2025 draft saw the Rams pick six players, with second-round pick TE Terrance Ferguson on IR and yet to contribute much. Third-round OLB Josaiah Stewart contributes here and there, but we'll see how much higher his ceiling is. Four day three picks aren't contributing and two of them are off the team. Then the Rams drafted five players in 2026 and none of them contribute. One didn't make the team and two are healthy scratches.

So these last two draft classes include 11 players and the only ones even really playing are Stewart and seventh-round receiver Konata Mumpfield, merely out of necessity because of injuries.

These are picks that we may look back on in 2027 and say "Good job, I didn't respect your vision Les Snead."

This would imply that first-round QB Ty Simpson is playing next year and looks like a worthy pick at 13, which was much higher than most experts believed he should go. This would mean that L.A.'s two second-round tight ends in the last two years (Ferguson, Max Klare) are playing like an exciting 13 personnel duo. This would mean that they don't keep parting ways with the guys they draft within 1-3 years, as they've been doing a lot of recently.

The trades (2 firsts for Stafford, 1 for McDuffie, 1+Verse for Garrett) make sense to me really. I think late first-round picks are overrated. But it's times like this being 4-4 and struggling in certain areas that definitely call into question the Rams' draft and trade philosophies because they haven't been to the Super Bowl since 2021 and have only come close once. The Rams need to see a better return on investment, and fast.

One more note: I've been one of the biggest Puka Nacua fans in the world since he debuted in 2023. The fact that he's not certainly part of L.A.'s future at this point is concerning, to say the least, both because of what Puka has done to make the team doubt him and because the Rams don't seem to realize that they'll be one of the worst teams in the NFL without him.

4) Who is one Rams player on offense and one on defense that Commanders fans probably don't know much about, but should?

Nothing on offense is going to surprise you. Stafford, Nacua, and Kyren Williams are still the big three. The only other player who pops is Corum and I just assume everybody knows about him by now because of fantasy football. Corum is a more dynamic runner than Williams, but McVay hasn't lost any trust in Kyren. There might be a few extra passes to Tutu Atwell this week.

Quentin Lake is someone who doesn't get enough credit as an actual star. He plays deep safety, linebacker, and slot, in addition to lining up nearly anywhere else. He has three picks and 11 passes defensed. Good against the run and the pass. Lake deserves as much credit for his value as Nick Emmanwori in Seattle.

5) How should Washington gameplan for this matchup on both sides of the ball?

Defensively, if you contain Williams and Corum on early downs with heavy fronts and get the Rams to third-and-medium+, you really limit Stafford's options because the offense is so one-dimensional without Davante Adams. Keep a linebacker or safety down in the box on those plays to try and take away Puka's underneath routes because despite Puka being a very talented downfield threat, most of his production actually comes on short passes. Force Stafford to use his secondary passing weapons like Tyler Higbee, Colby Parkinson, and Mumpfield because they're inconsistent and unreliable.

Offensively, use quick throw passing plays to get the ball out of Jayden Daniels' hands quickly and avoid third-and-long plays where the Rams pressure has a chance to get home. No free points, so protecting the ball is probably more important than scoring touchdowns–if you give the Rams enough time, they will end up making a mistake or two on special teams and turn the ball over once or twice. Those are Washington's best opportunities to capitlize.

#2860. Lakers star Luka Doncic joins LeBron James, issues strong cease and desist

Former Lakers teammates LeBron James and Luka Dončić are fighting a common opponent off of the court.

James is looking to get an AI sports betting app, which is named Bron AI, to stop using his likeness without authorization, a spokesperson for James confirmed to The California Post.

LeBron James #23 of the Philadelphia 76ers looks on during the game against the Chicago Bulls on November 2, 2026 at Xfinity Mobile Arena in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania. NBAE via Getty Images

Luka Doncic (77) of the Los Angeles Lakers stands for the national anthem before a game against the Portland Trail Blazers on Tuesday, Oct. 27, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

An attorney issued a cease-and-desist letter on the 76ers star's behalf to PlayVision Inc., the sports technology startup that owns Bron AI.

The advertisement for Bron AI that was posted to social media on Wednesday that led to the cease-and-desist shows a person meant to resemble James – wearing a No. 23 basketball jersey with the name "Bron" on it – flipping through the app.

The social media posts amassed over 900,000 views in less than 24 hours before being removed from X/Twitter after Front Office Sports first reported the cease-and-desist.

James, who left the Lakers for the 76ers in free agency this past summer, has an exclusive endorsement deal with Polymarket.

Basketball players Luka Doncic and LeBron James in Lakers jerseys. Los Angeles Times via Getty Images

Adam Mendelsohn, James's longtime media advisor, told The Post that Bron AI's ad is "another example of the decline and lack of originality in sports media."

"It's all about cheap clicks and aggregation," Mendelsohn said in a statement to The Post. "A lot of pundits do it too. I'm sure their intent all along was for us to send a cease and desist in order to play the social media game. You would prefer not to respond but you don't have a choice in a situation like this."

Bron AI's ad featured several athletes, including NBA players Giannis Antetokounmpo, Donovan Mitchell and Dillon Brooks.

A cease-and-desist on behalf of Dončić was also sent to PlayVision Inc., a source close to the Lakers superstar told The Post.

So, not everything is perfect for the Slovenian sharpshooter. Well, on the court it might be.

#2859. Trevor Lawrence is having by far the best season of his career

Trevor Lawrence has so much talent for the quarterback position that if freshmen could declare for the NFL draft, Lawrence probably would have been the first overall pick in 2019, when he had just led Clemson to a national championship as a true freshman. Instead, Lawrence stayed three years at Clemson and was the first pick in 2021, viewed as a sure-thing quarterback prospect.

In Year 6, Lawrence finally looks like everything he was supposed to be.

Lawrence is having by far the best year of his NFL career, with career-high stats across the board that are constrained only by the fact that the Jaguars win so many blowouts that Lawrence doesn’t throw many passes in the fourth quarter.

So far this season, Lawrence has completed 146 of 224 passes for 1,932 yards, with 18 touchdowns, four interceptions and an NFL-leading passer rating of 111.7. Project Lawrence’s totals to a 17-game season, and he’s on pace to finish with 4,106 yards, 38 touchdowns and nine interceptions. Those are MVP numbers.

Lawrence’s rookie year was a disaster, as he led the league in interceptions while playing on a chaotic Jaguars team coached by Urban Meyer, whose only foray into NFL coaching lasted less than one season. Lawrence and the Jaguars got a lot better once they kicked Meyer to the curb, but even in the next three years with Doug Pederson, an offensive coach with a Super Bowl-winning pedigree, Lawrence didn’t live up to the pre-draft hype.

Playing in Liam Coen’s offense, something has changed. Last year the Jaguars went 13-4, winning double-digit games for the first time since drafting Lawrence, and this year Lawrence and the Jaguars are even better. Lawrence’s average of 8.6 yards per pass is the best of his career by almost a yard and a half, a remarkable improvement for any quarterback to make in his sixth season.

Lawrence has thrown touchdown passes on 8 percent of his attempts, the best in the NFL this season and by far the best mark of his career. And after getting sacked a career-high 41 times last year, Lawrence has gotten much more comfortable reading the pass rush in his second year in Coen’s offense and has only been sacked 13 times this season.

Lawrence’s numbers would look even better if the Jaguars weren’t so often taking their foot off the gas late in games because they’re blowing opponents out. Lawrence has 1,196 yards and 12 touchdowns in the first halves of games this season, but he only has 736 yards and six touchdowns in the second halves of games.

The Jaguars currently have the fourth-best odds to win the Super Bowl, and Lawrence has the fifth-best odds to win MVP. That might be underselling them, and him. The Jaguars may just be the best team, with the best quarterback, in the NFL right now.

#2858. Texas high school football powerhouse considers legal action after playoff ban

Argyle Liberty Christian isn't going down without a fight after being kicked out of the TAPPS Division I football playoffs over an eligibility violation.

The Texas high school football powerhouse, considered a favorite to win the state championship, is considering legal action after TAPPS ruled Tuesday, Nov. 3, that the school used an ineligible player and must forfeit games, according to The Dallas Morning News.

"We strongly disagree with the TAPPS ruling and we provided clear and unambiguous evidence to TAPPS which demonstrated our innocence against the false assertions that were made against our football program," the school said in a letter to players' families.

The school called the ruling unfair and said it was exploring legal options to get its team reinstated.

The decision stems from a violation of TAPPS' "follow the coach" rule, which prohibits certain transfer students from competing at the varsity level if they were coached or trained by a coach at their new school within the previous 12 months.

Big wins send Clemens, Canyon Lake into E-N Top 20 football rankings

Multiple sources identified the player as wide receiver Jace Johnson, who transferred from Abilene Cooper before the 2026 season, according to The Dallas Morning News.

TAPPS determined that Johnson previously played on a 7-on-7 team coached by Liberty Christian assistant Antonio Griffin. The organization also said the school's athletic leadership and coaching staff knew or should have known about the connection.

The ruling could also end the high school football career of five-star linebacker Cooper Witten, an Oklahoma commit and son of former Dallas Cowboys star Jason Witten. His father coached Liberty Christian to consecutive TAPPS state championships in 2023 and 2024 before joining Oklahoma's coaching staff.

Cooper's mother, Michelle Witten, disputed the ruling on social media, saying Griffin never coached the player in question and that evidence supporting Liberty Christian was ignored.

TAPPS has revised its eight-team Division I playoff bracket, replacing Liberty Christian with TCA-Addison and delaying the opening round by one week.

Under the revised bracket, San Antonio Central Catholic will face Frisco Legacy Christian in the quarterfinals.

Liberty Christian's postseason hopes now depend on whether the school can successfully challenge the ruling.

#2857. Giants news: Cam Skattebo still sidelined, Malik Nabers wins monthly honor, more

Good morning, New York Giants fans! Here are the stories you need to know as the Giants get ready to face the Eagles in Philadelphia this Sunday.

This is inherited franchise history for John Harbaugh and the 2026 New York Giants, not earned.

But it's notable history nonetheless, resurfacing every time the Giants' buses hit the Turnpike and travel to what has become a house of horrors for this organization. The head coaches have changed. The players have changed. So have the circumstances.

The Eagles were supposed to win the NFC again. If they were going to fight for the title in the division, it was supposed to be with the Cowboys. Now those teams have a combined record of 5-11 and the Giants are talking about being buyers at the trade deadline instead of sellers, maybe even with Jonathan Taylor, one of the star running backs of the league, for a long time.

For now, it starts with McCarthy, clearly sold and mightily by one Harbaugh to another. Twenty-three years old. Giving Giants fans the kind of hope that J. Dart did last season. With a chance to be as great an in-season acquisition as the Giants have ever had if he keeps going like this, and if they keep going. Door wide open for the Michigan kid, and his coach, and his new team. They just have to make sure the Eagles don't slam it in their faces on Sunday.

John Harbaugh reaffirmed his confidence in kicker Dominic Zvada on Wednesday, saying the New York Giants are looking for consistent results but are not concerned about the position. The coach noted that the staff continues to evaluate technique and fundamentals, with the clear expectation that kicks will travel straight through the uprights.

"We're looking for kicks to go between the sticks, right through the upright, straight. Looking for straight kicks," Harbaugh told reporters. "But you're also looking at the technique and the fundamentals, but we're not worried about him."

Luxury target: Taylor might cost too much for a New York squad that's already down its fourth- and fifth-round picks in the 2027 draft, but what about Derrick Henry? If the Ravens decide their season is up without Lamar Jackson on the field, Henry could be a potential trade candidate. He's owed only $650,000 over the rest of 2026 and $12 million in 2027. And while the future Hall of Famer has struggled for most of the season, playing with a more mobile quarterback in McCarthy might help create some running lanes.

Bargain target: With the Chargers still winless, Harbaugh could call up former Ravens executive Joe Hortiz (and brother Jim Harbaugh) and attempt to make a move for Dalvin Tomlinson. The well-traveled defensive tackle has slipped as a pass rusher from his 2020-24 peak, but Tomlinson would be a rotational player for a Giants team that's barely getting by on the interior.

New York Giants: Defensive Interior. Given how they have played early in the season and the Philadelphia Eagles' struggles, the Giants have a serious chance of winning the NFC East. Since it is John Harbaugh's first year in New York, they probably shouldn't mortgage the future to get better this season, but they could add someone next to DJ Reader on the defensive line. Reader has earned a 90.4 PFF grade to lead all interior defensive linemen so far this season, but he is their only player at the position to have played at least 50 snaps and earned a PFF grade above 55.0.

External NFL sources, not from within the Giants building, vary in whether they believe a Taylor trade actually gets done. Several sources SNY touched base with are hellbent in their conviction that Harbaugh will do whatever he can to get him, seeing an opportunity for New York to make a run. Others, familiar with what the Colts are asking for, believe the price is so steep Indianapolis isn't actually interested in dealing him. He'll remain with them throughout this season, with a trade revisited in March.

Still, their stance to this point is the right one. Do everything you can to help your team accomplish that … within reason. They shouldn't waver. Harbaugh must fight the temptation to do more over the coming week.

#2856. 76ers' Joel Embiid assessed postgame flagrant 1 foul by NBA for kick to Pistons' Cade Cunningham

Philadelphia 76ers center Joel Embiid was assessed a postgame flagrant foul 1 for kicking Cade Cunningham in the first quarter of Wednesday's 112-106 win over the Detroit Pistons, the league announced on Thursday.

The play in question occurred at the 5:32 mark of the first quarter. With Cunningham defending him at the 3-point line, Embiid kicked out with his right leg on an initial shot attempt before passing to LeBron James. His kick struck Cunningham in the groin, causing the Pistons guard to bend over in pain.

Cunningham was slow to recover as Embiid cut past him for a dunk. He also trailed behind as play went to the other side of the court, grabbing the area that was struck. No foul was called on the play.

Whether it was the pain from the kick or perhaps frustration from no call being made, Cunningham only had nine points in the first half before finishing with 26 (shooting 1-for-9 on 3s) and 11 assists.

During Wednesday's game, Embiid was whistled for a technical foul for shoving Detroit's Tolu Smith under the basket with 1:25 remaining in the first half.

By NBA rules, a player assessed a flagrant foul 1 — defined as "unnecessary contact committed by a player against an opponent" — during the regular season receives one point for the infraction. Accumulating six points results in a one-game suspension. Wednesday's flagrant 1 penalty was the first for Embiid this season.

Two flagrant 1 fouls during a game results in ejection. A flagrant 2 comes with an automatic ejection.

Embiid tallied 26 points and 15 rebounds in the Sixers' win, which improved Philadelphia to 8-1 (4-0 on the road). Jaylen Brown led the 76ers with 30 points, adding 7 rebounds.

#2853. Jonah Coleman continues to turn heads with the Broncos

The rookie running back consistently takes advantage of the opportunities he gets on the field and runs with them, literally. In the 28-24 win over the Kansas City Chiefs, he had the game-changing reception that set up his game-winning touchdown.

Now comes the next chance for Coleman on Sunday against the Carolina Panthers and their 30th-ranked rushing defense.

When Broncos head coach Sean Payton was asked how Coleman has impressed despite the injury, Payton said:

"We had those glimpses in the spring and in training camp and in the preseason. Atlanta, there's a run, it just stood out to you. So the one thing that was unique about him is oftentimes you don't get to see them in third-down protection plans, but he was someone that we had quite a bit of tape on. Advanced protection plans, and he's built to handle the blitz.

"It's one thing to know who I have, but then also to be strong enough to do it. I would say that there were three backs — we just saw one of them that we really had visions for. Each one may be a little differently, but his versatility, to your point, that doesn't always — you don't get to stamp that with a lot of rookie backs that you feel like they're going to develop into. We saw enough that we felt like he was going to be a good third-down protecting back as well, with the way he was built."

In the victory over Kansas City, Coleman finished with 11 carries for 41 yards and a touchdown, as well as four receptions for 63 yards. Obviously, that total is boosted by the 51-yard reception to put Denver on the 4-yard line.

But it's clear the rookie brings an element to this offense that the other running backs on the roster cannot. Coleman has shown great vision, instincts, and runs with physicality that is fun to watch.

"I think he's playing really well with confidence," Broncos quarterback Bo Nix said to the media. "He's a very intelligent young running back. What sticks out the most is he can handle a lot. You can put a lot on his plate. After we learned that R.J. Harvey wasn't going to play last week, he got a little bit more of the bulk of R.J.'s stuff.

"It's impressive to see him be able to be used in a lot of different ways. When he gets in there, he's a natural runner. That's what he's drafted for. That's the position he plays. It's the out-of-the-backfield stuff that I think is making a good impression on us right now."

What's most impressive is how Coleman is handling pass protection. That's one of the toughest aspects for a rookie running back to grasp. What's exciting to contemplate is that Coleman can get even better the more he plays.

"We caught him in the right pressure there," Payto said to the media. "Yes, he's very comfortable. He's a different type (of) player than RJ (Harvey), but there were a lot of traits to like. More importantly, there was the exposure in an offense that sometimes the player just doesn't have that exposure, so you can't know for sure. I think we knew a little bit more with his tape."

All of this to say, Coleman brings a spark to the Broncos.

"I think that started since rookie minicamp, and then the preseason game against Atlanta, and then the Jacksonville game, and now this game," Broncos offensive coordinator Davis Webb said to the media. "I think every time he gets an opportunity, he's showed up. His teammates love him. He's smart, he's mature, and he's going to continue to get better, which is awesome."

#2850. Women's rights matter more than any league, say Afghan cricketers

Afghan women's cricketers have accused overseas players signing up for the inaugural Afghanistan Premier League of sending a message to the world that women suffering in the country "can simply be ignored".

Women's participation in sport has been effectively outlawed since the Taliban's return to power in 2021, and many of Afghanistan's female cricketers left the country for their own safety.

The men's franchise league is expected to start in December in the UAE, though it is yet to be sanctioned by the International Cricket Council (ICC).

In September, the England and Wales Cricket Board (ECB) urged players to reconsider their involvement and batter Ollie Pope withdrew.

"For five years, girls in Afghanistan have been denied the right to play cricket and many other basic rights," Nahida Sapan, who captained the Afghanistan Refugee Women's XI on this summer's tour of England, told the BBC Stumped podcast.

"Foreign players had a real opportunity to stand with millions of Afghan women and girls by choosing not to participate.

"It may have seemed like a small action, but it could have sent a powerful message and put pressure on those responsible.

"This is not about being against cricket or Afghan men's cricket. It is about standing up for basic human rights."

Eight English players were signed in the draft on Tuesday, alongside players from Scotland, Ireland, Australia and New Zealand.

England's Alex Hales, Tymal Mills, Joe Clarke, Olly Stone, Moeen Ali, Luke Wood, Tom Moores and Chris Wood were all signed. Scotland's George Munsey also secured a deal, as did Ireland all-rounder Curtis Campher.

Other England players including Dawid Malan and James Vince also put their names into the draft.

England will have more overseas representation than any other country in the Afghan league.

The major names signed from other nations include Sri Lanka internationals Kusal Perera, Wanindu Hasaranga, Dushmantha Chameera and Kamindu Mendis, Australian batter D'Arcy Short, South Africa spinner Imran Tahir and Bangladesh all-rounder Shakib Al Hasan.

The ECB has refused to schedule bilateral cricket with Afghanistan since the Taliban returned to power - a stance Australia also adopts.

England have, however, continued to play against Afghanistan at global events, including World Cups in both limited-overs formats and the Champions Trophy, which resulted in calls from MPs for their game in 2025 to be boycotted.

"For me, the women and girls of Afghanistan come first," said Sapan. "Their rights, dignity and freedom matter more than any league or sporting opportunity.

"Taking part in this league without acknowledging what is happening to Afghan women risks sending the message to the world that their suffering can simply be ignored."

Afghan women ask ICC for 'clear answers' on future

Afghan women tell the BBC their lives are unrecognisable after five years of Taliban rule

Afghanistan's women's players have repeatedly called on the ICC to recognise them once again as an international team, and commit to long-term funding.

Without official ICC recognition, they play as the Afghan Refugee Women's Team.

In April 2025, the ICC - together with the cricket boards of England, Australia and India - announced a joint programme to provide financial support for the players, and that funding was extended in July.

"To every international player considering participating in this league, I believe there is a responsibility to ask difficult questions," said batter Firooza Amiri.

"You have the freedom to choose where you play, which organisations you represent and which competitions you participate in, but that freedom also comes with a responsibility to understand the systems you may be helping to support.

"I am not asking international players to turn their backs on the people of Afghanistan. I am asking them to recognise the difference between supporting Afghan people and legitimising a system that has systematically taken away women's rights."

Afghanistan men's team has retained full ICC membership despite the country's inability to meet the membership requirement of supporting a women's national side.

Amiri said their stance was not about withdrawing support for the Afghanistan men's team.

"Imagine your daughter, your sister or your wife being told that she cannot attend school simply because she is a woman," she said. "Imagine being told that she cannot play sport, pursue her ambitions or participate in public life.

"We cannot celebrate the development of cricket in Afghanistan while ignoring the complete exclusion of half of its population from the game.

"The question is whether the international cricket community is prepared to stand by its own values when it comes to Afghan women."

#2848. Cavs reportedly sign Norchad Omier to two-way deal; waive Riley Minix

The Cleveland Cavaliers have signed G League standout Norchad Omier to a two-way deal. The organization has released 26-year-old wing Riley Minix in order to do so.

Omier previously had agreed to join the Minnesota Timberwolves but has reportedly changed his mind and plans on signing with the Cavaliers instead.

Minix, who signed a two-way deal with the Cavs in February of this year. The lanky forward never found his outside shot in limited opportunities with Cleveland.

All of this is easier said than done. Omier is not a polished player and isn't a threat to shoot from the perimeter. The 6'7" forward shot 20% from deep in 11 games with the Charge last season. But he has the size and athleticism to fill a momentary need for the Cavaliers — who are dealing with various injuries to a roster that already lacked wing depth.

Omier will have an opportunity now to continue proving himself in the G League, with a chance of earning minutes at the NBA level. This is what every prospect in his position is striving for. It's clear that he likes his chances in Cleveland, considering he backed out of a commitment with Minnesota to be here. The same can be said for the Cavs, who swiftly opened a two-way spot to get him.

The Cavs play two games at home this weekend. First, against the Philadelphia 76ers on Friday. Then, the New York Knicks on Saturday. We'll see if any of the two-way guys get a chance to play.

The sale of the Lakers to Kushner and Iger was reported in August, with the pair acquiring a majority stake in the team from owner Mark Walter amid a series of financial and legal difficulties for the billionaire. #2843. Thursday Night Football: Jaguars blow out Ravens 33-10
