#2824. Week 9 buy-low trade targets: Mahomes, Mason, Cooper, LaPorta at fantasy discounts

Fantasy football managers shopping for Week 9 buy-low trade targets should start with Chiefs quarterback Patrick Mahomes and Vikings running back Jordan Mason, two players whose trade prices have sunk compared to weeks past.

Mahomes gets a Jets team that has lost three straight, and backup Carson Wentz's start Monday against Buffalo points Minnesota toward a run-first plan. Jets rookie receiver Omar Cooper Jr. and Lions tight end Sam LaPorta also come at discounts after quiet Week 8 games.

MORE: Week 9 fantasy football PPR rankings: Burrow, Gibbs, Smith-Njigba lead

Kansas City Chiefs quarterback Patrick Mahomes (15) stands in the pocket against the Denver Broncos | Ron Chenoy-Imagn Images

Last week: 30-of-41, 321 yds, 3 TD, 28-24 L Broncos

This week: vs. Jets, Sun., Nov. 8, 1 p.m. ET

You may be thinking Mahomes' value is on the up after a plus performance. You'd be correct, but it's still down from where it was just a couple of weeks ago.

Mahomes went without a touchdown pass in the two games receiver Rashee Rice missed with a hamstring strain, then threw three in Rice's first game back. Rice caught seven passes for 53 yards and a touchdown in Denver, and he told reporters afterward that he's 100% healthy, which matters well beyond Sunday for an offense whose other receivers managed six catches for 39 yards at Seattle.

The Chiefs nearly won in Denver, too. Rice caught a 1-yard touchdown on the final snap, but a false start on right tackle Jaylon Moore wiped it out, and defensive tackle Malcolm Roach sacked Mahomes on the next play. The loss dropped the 5-2 Chiefs behind Denver in the AFC West, and Mahomes said Kansas City is now "in a dogfight for the division."

Rookie running back Emmett Johnson added a 73-yard catch-and-run touchdown, so Mahomes found a second big-play option in the same game. New York gave up 38 points to the Raiders last week, and the 31-year-old now has his top receiver back for a home game against a 2-6 team.

ROS FF Strength of Schedule: Three of Kansas City's next four opponents, the Jets, Cardinals and Bills, have allowed more fantasy points to quarterbacks than the NFL average over the last five weeks, and Arizona sits 29% above it. Atlanta in Week 10 and the Chargers in Week 17 are the toughest matchups left on the schedule.

MORE: Week 9 fantasy football QB start-sit: Willis, Shough, Darnold, Stroud

Detroit Lions defensive lineman Alim McNeill (54), left, and linebacker Malcolm Rodriguez (44), right, tackle Minnesota Vikings running back Jordan Mason (27) | USA TODAY Network via Reuters Connect (Reuters)

This week: vs. Bills, Mon., Nov. 9, 8:15 p.m. ET

Quarterback Kyler Murray strained his hamstring late in the loss to Detroit, and coach Kevin O'Connell ruled him out for Monday and called him week-to-week. Wentz won both of his appearances earlier this season, but he finished under 150 passing yards in each. Minnesota's passing game already ranks 31st in the league, while its defense allows the fewest points, so the Vikings have every reason to lean on the run.

Mason fits that plan. He was Minnesota's leading rusher before breaking his thumb after the opener, and O'Connell said before the Detroit game that the team wanted the former Georgia Tech back to be "a focal point" after running back Aaron Jones Sr.'s heavy workload. Jones has played through a sprained MCL since Week 2, and he aggravated it against Indianapolis, where Mason ran 15 times for 68 yards.

Mason still out-carried Jones 8-5 in Detroit, though the Lions held the Vikings' backfield to 46 rushing yards and a holding penalty erased a 29-yard run. Minnesota has been linked to veteran running backs in trade rumors, but O'Connell said he doesn't "necessarily see it as a depth issue at all."

ROS FF Strength of Schedule: Buffalo has allowed slightly more fantasy points to running backs than the NFL average over the last five weeks, and Weeks 12 and 13 bring Atlanta and Carolina, with the Falcons giving up nearly 34 points per game to the position. The tougher stretch comes in Weeks 14 and 15 against New England and Detroit, so a Wentz-led game plan should send most early-down carries to the 230-pound Mason while the matchups are favorable.

MORE: Week 9 fantasy football RB start-sit: Marks, Jones, Hall, Gordon

New York Jets wide receiver Omar Cooper Jr. (83) gains yards after catch during the first half against Tampa Bay Buccaneers | Vincent Carchietta-Imagn Images

This week: at Chiefs, Sun., Nov. 8, 1 p.m. ET

Receiver Garrett Wilson took a helmet-to-helmet hit from Raiders safety Jeremy Chinn late in the loss, reported concussion symptoms Monday and missed practice Wednesday and Thursday. Coach Aaron Glenn called him "day-to-day," and Wilson has drawn 27% of the Jets' targets, so his absence would push a large share of quarterback Geno Smith's throws toward Cooper and receiver Adonai Mitchell.

Cooper's zero-target game didn't come with a smaller role. He played 50 of the Jets' 64 snaps and ran as many routes as Mitchell, who caught seven passes and a touchdown.

The first-round pick missed four games with a high ankle sprain from Week 1, then posted 103 yards and a touchdown against New England and 115 yards and a touchdown against Miami. The Jets host Buffalo in Week 10, and the Bills have allowed 11 touchdowns to wide receivers in seven games.

ROS FF Strength of Schedule: The Jets host Buffalo in Week 10, and the Bills have allowed 17% more fantasy points to wide receivers than the NFL average over the last five weeks, after Kansas City held the position 16% below average over the same stretch. Most of New York's schedule after that sits near or below average, and Minnesota in Week 17 has allowed 44% fewer points than average to receivers. The former Indiana star averages 20.7 yards per catch on his 12 receptions, so he doesn't need heavy volume to produce in the matchups that do favor him.

MORE: Week 9 fantasy football WR start-sit: Allen, Addison, Douglas, Vele

Detroit Lions tight end Sam LaPorta (87) warms up ahead of the game against Green Bay Packers | USA TODAY Network via Reuters Connect (Reuters)

Last week: 3 rec., 33 yds on 5 tar., 19-15 W Vikings

This week: at Dolphins, Sun., Nov. 8, 1 p.m. ET

LaPorta's last two games came against division rivals. He caught one pass for 18 yards against Green Bay, then three for 33 against a Minnesota defense that allows the fewest points in the league. Even with that slide, the former Iowa star still ranks seventh among tight ends in PPR points after opening the season with 27 catches for 316 yards and three touchdowns in five games.

Miami is a much easier draw. The Dolphins have allowed the third-most fantasy points per game to tight ends, and Detroit's passing game gets them after facing two of the NFC's better defenses.

His contract hasn't affected his role. LaPorta, in the final year of his rookie deal, told "Pardon My Take" on Oct. 21 that the Lions are "not showing the love," but he said days later, "I want to play for this team."

ROS FF Strength of Schedule: Miami has allowed nearly 50% more fantasy points to tight ends than the NFL average over the last five weeks, and four of Detroit's next five opponents after that, New England, Tampa Bay, Chicago and Tennessee, sit at or above average. Minnesota and Green Bay remain the toughest tight end matchups on the schedule, but they don't arrive until Weeks 15 and 18.

MORE: Week 9 fantasy football TE start-sit: Kincaid, Dulcich, Pitts, Goedert

Each week's buy-low targets start with Yahoo Sports analyst Justin Boone's rest-of-season trade value charts, which we track over recent weeks to find players sitting at or near their lowest value of the season. From there, each candidate has to earn his spot with a reason his price should climb, whether that's an easier or more favorable strength of schedule, a bigger opportunity for targets and touches from an injury or depth chart change. Ultimately, we're seeking a guy whose low value is a mirage, and we want to buy in before his production improves.

#2823. #PostPulpit mailbag: Will the Patriots be buyers or sellers at the trade deadline?

It's a busy weekend in Foxboro as Rob Gronkowski and the 2016 Patriots are back in town, while the current New England Patriots squad has a big game themselves against the Green Bay Packers days before the trade deadline.

Before all the action begins, let's get into this week's edition of the #PostPulpit Mailbag.

Trade talk! Would they really trade Barmore? I could see it if they were 3-5, but seems like it would leave them very weak at a position that isn't terribly strong to begin with in a year when everything is still on the table in front of you. Maybe that's a move you make in the offseason, no? – RRDMuz

My belief entering this year was that this is Christian Barmore's last season in New England. Barmore has no guaranteed money remaining on his contract and the value of the deal has not matched his play since his return from blood clots. Pair that with long-term concerns over a lingering knee injury and it would make sense to move on.

As for the rest of this season, however, I'd still expect him and Carlton Davis to be on the roster at this time next week.

Where's the line between buy and sell? How do you think they see this beat-up team's chances for a deep playoff run? Moving Barmore weakens them badly for this year, so maybe if that's happening it's not worth adding pieces? If it doesn't, maybe some depth? Or do they just stay the course and any playoff appearance or win becomes gravy? – ed.liebfried

With Drake Maye and Mike Vrabel, the Patriots will always have a chance to compete and make a run in the playoffs. When it comes to selling, I do think there's a logical argument for getting younger and adding draft capital, but the time and place for that is next offseason.

At this deadline, I think the Patriots will focus on finding minor upgrades to bolster the roster. The best-case scenario would be finding the next version of Kyle Van Noy, who they acquired in exchange for a late-round pick swap.

And while I hate when teams like the Red Sox use injured players returning as a reason not to be active at the deadline, I think it's a different story in football. The Patriots getting players back like Mack Hollins, Romeo Doubs and Alijah Vera-Tucker in upcoming weeks are a significant boost to the roster.

What's up with Henderson and the trade talk? Not a culture fit? Or system fit?? It seems odd he's not even practicing – Groganator

Henderson has now missed over a week with a legitimate illness, which is why he has been absent. The trade speculation stems from some of his struggles this season, but I wouldn't be ready to give up on the 24-year-old second-round pick, who posted over 1,100 yards and 10 touchdowns last season.

The hope should be that Henderson can get healthy from both his illness and ankle injury and that the coaching staff can find a way to get him going on the field.

If this team is fully healthy where would you look to improve – XGhost-PepperX

As we discussed, we are expecting more minor additions to the roster. With wide receivers and linemen coming back from injury, a blocking tight end would be the main spot we'd look on offense. Part of the run game issues have been with the tight ends blocking the edges as that is not Hunter Henry's game and Eli Raridon has had up-and-down moments like most rookies.

On defense, the defensive line has played better in recent weeks but a true nose tackle and edge rusher — as they still only three on the roster — would be players to add. Another linebacker to allow the defense to play more true base defense against heavier personnel groupings could also be a target.

Williams, Douglas, and Chism were productive against Miami. Was that because it was Miami? Or, in the playoffs, might we have 6-8 skill position players who are dangerous? – WannaBe Pats GM

We've been in the camp that all three are quality wide receivers in this league. They might not be bonafide No. 1 options, but they all have unique skillsets that make that strong complimentary pieces who can also take on larger roles when players above them are injured.

We'll also continue to push for more Kyle Williams snaps, specifically in the slot like he was used in college. He's a dangerous player with his speed and the Patriots should look to exploit that more.

Will the New England Patriots activate both WR Mack Hollins and FB Reggie Gilliam this week? – For Pats Sake

Hollins looks ready to go. Gilliam isn't ready yet but has increased his presence in the locker room in the past week.

With so many QB injuries around the league, do you think one of our QBs have some value to bring good draft value (not a sixth round) or some one we can exchange with? – sumeetw

Tommy DeVito has started games in the league so could have some attraction. But, I'd say the injuries around the league show how valuable it is to have depth at that position. Any quality backup quarterback is more valuable for a competing team compared to a late-round draft pick.

That's all for this week's #PostPulpit mailbag. If you have questions you'd liked to be answered next week, submit them online in our weekly submission post on the Feed. Make sure to follow @iambrianhines and @PatsPulpit as well.

#2822. 2026-27 NHL season predictions: Expert picks for Stanley Cup playoffs, champion and major awards this season

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Following a brief offseason and an expedited preseason, the 2026-27 NHL season has arrived. Before the puck drops on opening night, we have some predictions to make.

If this season feel like it's staring earlier than usual, that's because it is. The NHL is adopting an 84-game regular season, which just means more hockey for the rest of us. That also means a long and arduous journey to the Stanley Cup is ever so slightly more difficult.

So, what will that journey look like? The Carolina Hurricanes are looking to defend their title as Cup champions, but they'll have to contend with a deep Eastern Conference and some real juggernauts in the Western Conference. With that in mind, we logged our expert predictions for the playoff field in each conference -- and there were a few surprises -- before making our Stanley Cup Final selections.

We also called our shot on the winners of each major individual award. Connor McDavid is looking for his fourth Hart Trophy, but he's got some competition from an up-and-coming superstar in his own division. Races for the Norris Trophy and Vezina Trophy should be pretty wide open, and unexpected candidates always emerge throughout the season.

It should be a fun season in the NHL, so follow along with all our coverage at CBS Sports. Let's get things kicked off with the season preview and predictions, which will of course be 100% accurate.

Baumgartner: I don't see many new faces entering the conversation when it comes to the Eastern Conference playoff race. A healthy Florida Panthers side will slot themselves right back towards the top of the Atlantic, and a revamped Washington Capitals side should be able to make some noise in the Metropolitan. Besides that, I have six 2026 playoff teams returning to the dance in 2027.

Nivison: As much as I hate to go with chalk in preseason predictions -- because it rarely happens that way -- there's no denying the top dogs in the West. The Avalanche and Stars are the class of the Central Division, and the Golden Knights should pound a pitiful Pacific Division into a pulp. Things get more interesting behind those clear Cup contenders, and I'm projecting another significant leap forward from the Mammoth. I also think Macklin Celebrini will be successful in dragging the Sharks to the playoffs in his third NHL season.

Nivison -- Columbus Blue Jackets: The Blue Jackets have been agonizingly close to the playoffs in each of the last two seasons, and I think they get over the hump in 2026-27. There are still questions in Columbus. Can Adam Fantilli be a franchise player at center? Is the forward group as a whole good enough to keep pace in a competitive Eastern Conference? Still, I am sure the Blue Jackets have a really strong blue line, led by Norris Trophy winner Zach Werenski, and goaltender Jet Greaves is on track to become a Vezina Trophy candidate. Internal improvements from the young core will then get Columbus above the cut line.

Baumgartner -- San Jose Sharks: The Sharks narrowly missed out on the 2026 Stanley Cup Playoffs, finishing just four points short of the second wild card. San Jose added a good group of veterans over the summer, but the most important offseason move was selecting Ivar Stenberg with the No. 2 pick in the Draft. Stenberg joins the youth movement in San Jose, which is spearheaded by new captain Macklin Celebrini, who broke the Sharks single-season points record with 115 last season. The Sharks are a better team than they were last year, and with Celebrini entering his third season as a legit Hart Trophy contender, this team won't be fun to play against.

Nivison: Yes, the Mammoth have a brutal path coming out of the Central Divison, but just close your eyes and imagine this scenario with me. Logan Cooley becomes a superstar center. Dylan Guenther hits 50 goals. Dmitry Simashev asserts himself as a budding top-four defenseman. Anders Lee and Vincent Trocheck provide necessary veteran depth. Now open your eyes. Utah is your Western Conference champion.

Unfortunately for the Mammoth, they will run into a refreshed Panthers team that now has double-barreled Tkachuks. Last season, we found out that all it takes to stop Florida is to hope all of its key players miss significant time with injury. The long offseason was probably a blessing in disguise as it allowed the Panthers to get healthy, and that's bad news for everyone else. The structure in front of the crease will limit some of the volatility we saw from Jacob Markstrom in New Jersey, and Florida wins its third Stanley Cup in four years.

Baumgartner: Before last season, the Carolina Hurricanes' kryptonite was the Eastern Conference Final. The Hurricanes were able to overcome their demons and win the East before ultimately taking home the franchise's first Stanley Cup since 2006. In the West, the Dallas Stars have been running into the same problem. After three straight seasons ending in the Western Conference Final, the Stars regressed last postseason, flaming out in the first-round to the Minnesota Wild. The Central Division will once again be a gauntlet, but I'm ready for Dallas to take the next step and reach the Stanley Cup Final.

While Dallas has continued to fall short in recent years, the Panthers have done the opposite. The Panthers made three consecutive Stanley Cup Final appearances, winning back-to-back Cups in 2023-24 and 2024-25. Last season, Florida's three-peat attempt came to a halt on day one of training after captain Aleksander Barkov went down with a non-contact injury, tearing both the ACL and MCL in his right knee. An offseason of healing for Barkov, and the rest of the banged up Panthers, has the team refreshed entering September for the first time in years. With nearly the entire 2025 Stanley Cup winning core still on the team and Brady Tkachuk added to the fold, Florida looks ready to win its third Stanley Cup in four seasons.

Nivison: Celebrini should have won the Hart Trophy last season, but for some incomprehensible reason, he wasn't even a finalist. This year, Celebrini replicates that performance and gets San Jose to the postseason to take home his first MVP award at age 20. Hughes posts a career year in his first full season with the Wild, and Spencer Carberry gets his flowers for winning the Metro Division after missing the playoffs last year.

Baumgartner: McDavid's ability to take over a game was evident nearly every time he touched the ice last season. His 24 points (11 goals,13 assists) in the final 14 games of the regular season -- without Leon Draisaitl in the lineup -- was one of the many reasons I put him No. 1 on my Hart Trophy ballot. With just two years left on his current contract, desperation may start to kick in to get the job in Edmonton, which would lead to another MVP-caliber season. Barkov is healthy and the frontrunner to win his fourth Selke, and Dahlin will have another strong season to take home his first career Norris Trophy.

Atlanta Falcons running back Brian Robinson. Mandatory Credit: Jeff Hanisch-Imagn Images

#2820. Warriors star fires back at Kendrick Perkins: ‘The difference between myself and big dummies like you is that I can think’

Draymond Green heard Kendrick Perkins' comments loud and clear.

The former NBA champion-turned-analyst called the Warriors veteran a liability after he was ejected from a game against the Suns. Green wasn't happy with Perkins' assessment and immediately fired back.

Draymond Green #23 speaks to Stephen Curry #30 of the Golden State Warriors on the bench during the second half of their game against the LA Clippers at Chase Center on November 01, 2026 in San Francisco, California. Getty Images

"I'm not even close to the player I once was. But Kendrick Perkins, the difference between myself and big dummies like you is that I can think," Green said on his podcast.

"When your body wasn't the same no more or as athletic as you once were, it looked a little different for you because you weren't the smartest basketball player."

Green received two technicals on one play, and his heated interactions with Suns guard Dillon Brooks went viral on social media. The two attempted to put their arms around each other and had to be separated.

Green's ejection was discussed on ESPN's "First Take" the following morning, and Perkins didn't hold back.

Kendrick Perkins is seen prior to the first round of the 2026 NBA Draft at Barclays Center on June 23, 2026, in New York. Getty Images

"When was the last time we discussed Draymond Green and brought him up for his basketball and not fighting? At some point, he has to deliver on the floor," Perkins said.

"And when you look at the start of this season, Draymond Green has been nonexistent. He has done absolutely nothing for the Golden State Warriors. To me, he has been a liability more than an asset."

Green has struggled offensively to start the season, averaging 3.2 points per game. At 36 years old, his defensive prowess has declined, but he remains a talented veteran because of his basketball IQ.
